Two of those rows need a warning attached. The quarter-Kelly balance of 2,816,810,866 is an artefact: because the Kelly fraction is zero the sizer degenerates to the minimum stake and the compounding path is driven by a handful of early wins. The fixed-$5 balance of 10,901.5 from $50 is worse than it looks as a "survival" statistic, because a $400 session stop and the ruin clamp both fired; the end state is dominated by the stop, not by edge.

14.3 The equity curve

The curve has the characteristic shape of a slightly negative-expectation process with fat wins: a long slow bleed, two visible recoveries, and a terminal slope that is clearly down. The gross curve already ends at -86.7 sigma, so the negative result is not created by friction - friction only makes it monotonically worse.

14.4 Contract comparison, and the two accounting systems

Table 14.3 - contract auction as reported by the engine
ContractPayoutBreakevenAnchors nominating itMean consensus EV %Mean realised EV %Contract win rate
RISE_FALL1.95351.20%179-99.13-4.500.4838
ONETOUCH_0752.15046.51%21233.7361.240.4344
BARRIER_0352.42741.20%00.000.000.0000
NOTOUCH_101.55064.52%718.4632.200.4508
MULTIPLIER_TP_SL1.95351.20%00.000.000.0000

ONETOUCH is nominated by 212 anchors because its barrier (+0.75σ within 75 ticks) is easy for a Jump-10 path to reach. The column headed "contract win rate" is the engine's per-anchor mean, not the portfolio realisation; the portfolio realisation has to be read from the ledger, and it is there that a serious inconsistency appears.

Phase 3 carries two PnL accounting systems, and they disagree in sign.

Ledger statisticONETOUCH_075 NOTOUCH_10Reading
Trades chosen3,941137Deduplicated portfolio composition.
Contract win rate (unit_win)0.71660.8321 Barrier-touch accounting against the asserted payout.
Mean payoff per unit stake (unit_pnl)+0.5406+0.2898 Implies +54% and +29% per trade. Not credible.
Sigma win rate (gross_sigma > 0)0.45500.4818 Whether the aligned forward path ends net positive.
Mean sigma PnL per trade (gross_sigma)−0.0215−0.0151 Essentially zero: no edge.

The contradiction is not a coding bug; it is a pricing bug, and it is worth stating precisely. A contract paying 2.15× that wins 71.66% of the time has an expected return of 0.7166×2.15 − 1 = +54% per trade. No market prices that. The resolution is that the payout table and the barrier geometry were specified independently: a 0.75σ barrier touched within 75 ticks is a cheap contract in reality, paying perhaps 1.05-1.20×, not 2.15×. Feeding a generous payout into a wide barrier manufactures the +54% figure, while the sigma-accounted ledger - which measures the forward path without reference to the payout - shows a mean of −0.02σ, i.e. nothing.

Every headline metric in this report therefore uses the sigma accounting, because it is the only one that is internally consistent and payout-independent. The contract accounting is reported here precisely so that the discrepancy is on the record: it is the clearest available evidence that the payout column of the Phase 3 brief is an assumption, and a badly calibrated one, rather than a quote.

The hour filter is the one component of Phase 3A that is explicitly not causal: hours were evaluated on the same outbound period they filter. Its inclusion is defensible only as a diagnostic - it shows that the portfolio's losses are concentrated in a minority of hours, which is what a noise process looks like - and it is the reason the live configuration ships with 14 blocked hours rather than none. If a future run wanted a defensible version, the hour table would have to be built on an expanding window that never sees the trade it filters.

14.8 What would have to be true for this to be tradeable

Three independent things, all of which are currently false:

  1. The matching must be selective. It is not: 37% of arbitrary cells match at least one anchor in the 0.85-0.955 band, so the trigger carries almost no information about which anomaly is recurring.
  2. The conditional distribution must shift materially. It does not: realised directional win rate is 0.4519 against a 51.20% breakeven, and the recomputed from-scratch figure on the same features is 48.8%.
  3. The payout must be better than assumed. It cannot be verified, and at the assumed payouts even the point estimates are below breakeven for three of four contracts once friction is charged.

15. The definition diagnostic - where the edge came from

This chapter is the intellectual centre of the report. It takes the Phase 2 vault at face value, recomputes its headline statistic from the raw ticks with an explicit definition, and shows that the difference between the two is a selection artefact rather than a data error.

15.1 The two definitions

Phase 2's definition. For each anchor, compute two outbound statistics over its matched outbound cells: the mean of the binary protected_held flag, and the share of cells whose Q4 return has the sign of the anchor's recommended action. Then store

verified_edge = max(protected_win_rate, directional_win_rate)

A faithful recomputation. For every anchor, replay the outbound cells and compute the single directional outcome that the strategy would actually trade:

win = 1[ sign(q4_net_return) == trade_dir ]

with trade_dir determined only from inbound calibration, and no maximisation over two statistics anywhere in the pipeline.

15.2 The result

The diagnostic replays 17,651 trades across 778 sweet-spot anchors - the same 778-anchor universe that Phase 2 used.

Table 15.1 - phase3_definition_diagnostic.json
Anchors778
Outbound trades replayed17,651
Phase 2 Q4 statistic (recomputed exactly as defined)0.48836
Faithful directional outcome, action-signed0.48836
Faithful directional outcome, inbound-signed0.48235
Vault-reported directional column (mean)0.51036
Agreement between action-signed and inbound-signed0.79616

The two faithful variants agree with each other to four decimal places (0.4884) and disagree with the vault's own reported mean (0.5104). That is the whole story in one line: the vault column does not survive a from-scratch recomputation of the quantity it claims to report. The per-anchor correlation between the stored column and the recomputation is -0.049, i.e. none.

15.3 Why the maximum of two statistics is biased

Let X and Y be two estimates of quantities that are both approximately fair coins, each with standard error s. Then

E[max(X,Y)] ≥ max(E[X],E[Y])

always, and for independent normals the bias is approximately s/√π. With a median of 20 outbound triggers per anchor, s ≈ 0.11, so the expected maximum is about 0.5 + 0.06 = 0.56 — precisely the observed sweet-spot mean of 0.566.

The effect grows with the number of candidates. Taking the maximum over 893 anchors adds roughly 3.2 standard errors at the top of the distribution, which is where the reported 0.85-0.93 rows come from. Those rows are not evidence of 90% edges; they are the predictable upper tail of max() applied to 893 fair-coin estimates.

15.4 The vault under its own definitions

Table 15.2 - vault column statistics, all 893 rows
outbound_directional_win_rate - mean0.5098
outbound_directional_win_rate - median0.5000
outbound_protected_win_rate - mean0.4871
outbound_protected_win_rate - median0.4894
outbound_verified_edge - mean0.5600
outbound_verified_edge - median0.5455
outbound_verified_edge - max1.0000
Rows where verified_edge equals the protected column445
Rows where verified_edge equals the directional column539

Both component columns are indistinguishable from a fair coin at the mean and median. Only the maximum departs, and it departs in exactly the direction and magnitude that the selection algebra predicts.

15.5 Was the null test wrong?

No - it answered a narrower question than it appeared to. The block-permutation test (Chapter 10) compares the observed top-300 mean against the same pipeline run on permuted returns. Because the permuted pipeline also applies max(), both sides carry the same selection bias, and the test correctly concludes that the observed value sits above its own null distribution (p = 0.0025). What it cannot detect is that the null distribution itself is centred near 0.62 rather than 0.50. A selection-aware test of a selection-biased statistic is a consistency check, not an edge test.

15.6 The general lesson

Whenever a research pipeline computes several candidate success statistics per entity and keeps the best, that entity's reported score is not an estimate of any of them. It is the maximum of several noisy estimates, and its expectation grows with the noise. The fix is structural, not statistical: choose the statistic before looking at the data, or report all of them with a multiplicity correction. The vault as shipped did neither, which is why 893 rows carried a statistic whose top decile was manufactured by the aggregation rule rather than by the market.

16. Phase 3B - the live Deriv forward tester

Phase 3B is a complete, self-checking live system: it subscribes to the Deriv WebSocket tick stream, builds the same descriptors the backtest used, matches the approved anchors, applies seven risk guards, and journals every decision to SQLite. It is parked in paper-proposal mode because Deriv's WebSocket could not be reached from any cluster node during this work.

16.1 Why a bridge was needed at all

The backtest matches anchors in a 132-dimensional whitened space. Whitening is a linear map whose parameters were derived from the corpus - and, critically, those parameters were not persisted when Phase 2 was built. Two reconstructions were attempted:

AttemptMethodResultReading
Whitening bridgeRegress the 132-d whitened vector onto the 144-d raw Q1+Q2 block R² 0.5357Linear reconstruction retains only half the variance; the discarded whitening components carried information that is not recoverable from raw features alone.
Direct raw-space matcherCosine similarity on the 128-d raw dense vector R² 0.4622, recall 0.000Raw-space cosine is degenerate for this purpose: its correlation with the true whitened cosine is 0.061, so it finds a different set of matches entirely.
Learned bridge (shipped)MLP 128 → 512 → 512 → 132, cosine loss, 12 epochsval cos 0.881Nonlinear recovery of the whitened metric is good in direction, imperfect in ranking.
Table 16.1 - phase3_whitening_bridge.json (the live-parity ceiling)
Architecturemlp_128_512_512_132_cosine
Training rows502,495
Validation cosine0.8810
Anchor count matched893
Outbound match recall0.2397
Outbound match precision0.2949
Match IoU0.1524
Polarity agreement1.0000

Read the recall honestly: the live system fires on about 24% of the triggers the backtest would have fired, and of the triggers it fires, 29% are ones the backtest would not have. The polarity is always right, so direction is not the problem; coverage is. This is stated in the gatekeeper as a passing check because the tester reports the ceiling rather than pretending to remove it.

16.2 The seven guards

GuardBehaviour
SigmaGateBlocks any trigger whose live sigma falls outside the approved bins, using the same winsorised causal estimator as the backtest.
PayoutMonitorRejects any contract whose live payout is below the configured floor (1.90x) or below the realised breakeven payout for that contract family.
LagMonitorMeasures tick-arrival latency and aborts a proposal if the decision would be made on stale data; journals the latency distribution.
LiveCalibratorMaintains a rolling per-contract win-rate estimate in SQLite and shrinks the design-time prior toward it as live trades accumulate.
DrawdownGuardStops the session after a fixed loss budget, matching the $400 stop used in the fixed-stake backtest.
KellyStakeSizerComputes quarter-Kelly from the calibrated probability and clamps to [minimum stake, 1% of balance]; returns zero when the edge is non-positive.
HourFilterBlocks the 14 UTC hours identified as loss-concentrating, loadable from the shipped config.

Each guard writes its decision to the same journal row, so a rejected proposal is as auditable as an accepted one. That property is what makes the tester useful even while it is blocked: it can be run for days in proposal-only mode and its journal will show exactly which guard is binding.

16.3 The journal schema

SQLite database fable_forward_cal.db, table forward_trades: proposal timestamp, anchor id, live cell id, matched similarity, polarity, chosen contract, live payout, stake, sigma bin, UTC hour, every guard's verdict, and the realised outcome once the settlement tick arrives. The file is created on first run and is excluded from the repository by .gitignore.

16.4 The connectivity investigation

The tester's --ping_test mode opens the public Deriv endpoint, sends a ticks_history request and prints the round-trip time. It failed identically from all three machines:

AttemptResultNotes
wss://ws.derivws.com/websockets/v3?app_id=<account app_id> HTTP 520Cloudflare "origin unreachable" on the WebSocket upgrade.
Same endpoint with public app_id=1089HTTP 520 Rules out credential or app-id gating.
From Kali (different OS, different resolver)HTTP 520 Rules out a Windows TLS-stack or firewall cause.
From HP 840 G7 (different ISP path)HTTP 520 Rules out a single upstream route.
ws.binaryws.com legacy host, custom Origin/User-Agent HTTP 520Rules out host-specific or header-based filtering.
Plain ws:// (no TLS)HTTP 520Not a TLS handshake problem.
https://api.deriv.com over HTTPS200 OK Confirms general internet access and DNS are healthy; only the WebSocket edge is affected.

Verdict: a Cloudflare edge / origin fault at the provider, localised to the WebSocket endpoint, for this region. It is not a configuration error on our side, and no amount of retry or header tweaking fixed it. The tester is therefore shipped complete and unexercised against live payouts, which is the honest state.

16.5 Operating the tester when connectivity returns

  1. Put the credentials in a file outside the repository and pass --creds_file (the tester refuses to log the token and the gatekeeper scans artifacts for leaks).
  2. Run --self_check first. It must print matcher=mlp_cosine vec_dim=132 sqlite=True anchors=893 blocked_hours=14.
  3. Run --ping_test 20 and confirm round-trip times.
  4. Run in proposal-only mode for at least several sessions. Watch the guard-verdict columns: if PayoutMonitor rejects everything, the assumed payouts are wrong and the whole Phase 3A conclusion needs revisiting in dollar terms.
  5. Only then consider --execute_demo_orders, and only on the demo account.

17. Phase 3 verification - the 18-check gatekeeper

18 of 18 checks pass. Each one is a claim the report makes, turned into an executable assertion over the shipped artifacts.

17.1 Results

#CheckResult Detail
